## 1. Purpose, self-limitation and reading order

After that review it may be a reviewed continuation candidate; it is never self-authenticating governance and never supersedes the controlling protocol. Original P1 is RECOVERED CONVERSATION EVIDENCE, not implemented bytes. Its full authenticated report is preserved in Appendix R1. Historical states E0 AVAILABLE → E1 RESERVED → E2 VALIDATED → E3 COMMITTED → E4 protected exit are distinct from later B/S states. Historical contract: runtime/profile eligibility and rejection of pre-existing Python error before frame inspection/E1; E1 before ancestry scan; same reservation and GIL continuously through semantic validation/E2/E3; public frame APIs only; exact canonical-code census; initial NULL rejects, never empty ancestry; clean terminal NULL required for exhaustion; canonical match rejects before E2/E3; no internal frame read or pending-state mutation. No signal/pending processing or GIL release occurs in the protected scan; native signal recording may continue.

IR0 code temporaries are released immediately after comparison. Successor frame is acquired before predecessor release. Rejection discharges IR0 before E1 release. All inspection references are discharged before E2.

## 18. P1-B materialization, IR0 and errors

P1-B: frame materialization MAY allocate, but the audited exact CPython 3.14.5 admitted profile does not execute Python or run GC collection on those paths. Allocation may schedule later GC. This is source/architecture reasoning, not executed qualification, and does not generalize to free-threaded builds, arbitrary versions, allocators, reference tracers or runtime hooks.

Public inspection APIs: `PyThreadState_GetFrame`, `PyFrame_GetBack`, `PyFrame_GetCode`, exact type/code identity checks, proved IR0 `Py_DECREF`, and the reviewed Python error observation/transfer operations. Initial getter NULL rejects as ancestry unavailable; its implementation can clear a materialization error, so no fabricated OOM diagnosis. Back getter NULL with clear indicator is clean end; with error it is traversal failure. Never accept a truncated prefix.

Existing error rejects before E1 and is preserved. For traversal failure, transfer/preserve the inspection exception, discharge IR0, release E1, then restore the same exception with an empty destination indicator; no protected formatting/normalization. `PyErr_SetRaisedException` can decrement the replaced error, so destination-empty is material. Canonical match uses fixed native rejection evidence, safe IR0 cleanup and synchronous reservation release before Python resumes.

The executing interpreter/generator frame independently owns each materialized live-ancestry frame while GIL/no-Python/no-teardown preserves that ancestry. Returned code also has its independent live executable owner. Scanner releases are non-last; no frame/locals destruction, weakref/finalizer, GC collection or error-state mutation follows. Complete source/API/error inventory and pinned-source references remain in the audit appendix.

## 20. NEW prospective T1 completion and exact successful sequence

OPTION T1 — RELEASE AT SCANNER COMPLETION is a NEW prospective architecture choice. T2 (retain final frame through semantic validation, release before E2) was technically viable under historical P1, not unsafe; it was not selected because it adds unnecessary semantic-path cleanup ownership.

1. Inspect/compare current code.
2. Release the returned code immediately under IR0.
3. Acquire back-frame result while retaining current frame.
4. Valid successor: release predecessor under IR0 and continue.
5. Terminal NULL: establish clear error indicator and complete clean exhaustion.
6. Release final current frame exactly once under IR0.
7. Establish all scanner frame/code owning slots empty; no inspection exception owned.
8. Complete E1-A.
9. Enter E1-B immediately with the same lifetime, attempt, E1, P0 and GIL.
10. Complete canonical H1 validation and required canonical temporary cleanup.
11. Publish E2 only after both regions succeed.
12. Permit E3 only by its separate same-attempt one-shot transition.

Minimum-lifetime rule adopted prospectively ONLY for P1 inspection ownership: release when inspection no longer needs the reference while the IR0 independent-owner proof remains valid. Do not generalize to R0/LR0 or Registry-root ownership. Semantic validation needs no final frame; continuous exclusion preserves ancestry correctness after release. No new semantic authority, detection semantics, writer admission, reservation or governance change is introduced.

## 21. Completed E1-A / E1-B interface

E1-A is reserved ancestry validation INCLUDING complete IR0 cleanup. Audited frame materialization may allocate; P1-specific error semantics apply. E1-B is canonical CS-R1 validation under its exact allocation-free/error-preserving admitted whitelist. Neither region permits Python bytecode/callback/finalizer/weakref execution, GC collection, GIL release, signal checks or pending-call processing.

Transition requires complete clean scan, no prohibited canonical match, zero IR0 owning temporaries, no locally owned inspection exception and clear error state. Same E1/P0/GIL/lifetime/attempt and writer exclusion remain uninterrupted. No return to Python, release/reacquisition or wait-for-Python step. Fixed evidence and checked counters prevent unbounded observation growth; closed profile forbids new work/replacement. Registry can retain other Associations. ## 42. Full historical report preservation — provenance and precedence

Appendices R1–R6 preserve complete recovered report text. They are evidence transcripts, not current execution instructions. Their historical authentication claims describe their own tasks, not fresh checks here. Their historical unresolved statuses/next gates must be read chronologically through the later T1 completion and current synthesis. R2 supplies exact selected native machinery and matrix; R3 audits P1 APIs; R4 preserves the absence of a historical choice; R5 explicitly makes the new choice. R6 is only a historical publication response, not missing V7 raw files.

No transformation below is claimed to reproduce a raw V7 file. Presentation trims trailing whitespace and adds Markdown blockquote prefixes; original extraction hashes remain those in §40.

### Appendix R1. Historical P1 report

**HISTORICAL REPORT / ORIGINAL PROVENANCE RETAINED.** Current status and authorization are in §§22, 36–38 and the bootstrap.


> **P1 — public CPython APIs are sufficient with a pre-established native association/exclusion record.** No direct internal-frame read is required for the bounded profile.
>
> The prospective solution uses conservative code-identity rejection, a provisional native reservation, and a distinct one-shot finalization commit. This is a **CORRECTED-CANDIDATE architecture input**, not independent review, implementation authorization or qualification.
>
> **Authentication and preservation**
>
> Authentication passed:
>
> - Repository `/Users/erale/Documents/orev3`; branch/upstream `research/post-v1` / `origin/research/post-v1`.
> - Local, tracking and independently queried live-remote HEAD: `6f4c59e3871cf12c3bfc70e7ebad0dd88fb867f0`.
> - Ahead/behind **0/0**; index empty.
> - Mutable boundary **5 tracked + 31 untracked = 36**.
> - Protocol/checkpoint/source/test identities matched.
> - All **703 preservation identities/modes** matched.
> - Architecture report/manifest and receipt-authority bindings matched.
> - `git diff --check` passed.
>
> No repository changes, candidate execution, tests, signals, experiments or builds occurred.
>
> **Current mechanism and prospective difference**
>
> [_Ledger._admit()](/Users/erale/Documents/orev3/src/orev3/execution/cancellation_capability.py:752) inspects the current live ancestry, canonical code identities, owner locals and admission markers. It rejects competing runners and receipt/runner overlap. Its lifetime is the executing frame; a traceback retaining an escaped frame does not retain admission. No Python `finally` must release a lock.
>
> The prospective native admission preserves that principle but uses a **more conservative predicate**:
>
> > Any live frame matching the finite canonical ingress/continuation census blocks finalization, regardless of its owner locals or whether its Python marker has executed.
>
> This eliminates frame-local inspection. It does not alter existing I3/I4 admission semantics.
>
> The bounded topology permits one canonical source owner for the admitted process-global signal configuration. Rejecting a canonical frame belonging to another owner is safe contention rejection, not evidence that the admitted owner failed.
>
> **Finite frame census**
>
> Code objects are retained and authenticated before source installation. Symbols below identify exact functions in the current bytes or explicitly identified prospective functions.
>
> | Frame | Role and participation | Pre-marker treatment | Finalization rule |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | Prospective F | Runtime callback delegating canonical ingress or post-cut ordinary routing | Reject from publicly visible execution entry, before its first marker | Always blocks while live |
> | Prospective finalization wrapper | Canonical caller of native admission | Exact expected entry wrapper is allowed once | A second live wrapper rejects reentry |
> | `_Ledger._receive` | Receipt/classification | Reject entire frame, not only `_ingress=True` interval | Blocks |
> | `_Ledger._admit` | Runner admission inspection | Not itself receipt authority | Blocks conservatively while live |
> | `_Ledger._prepare` | Preparation mutation | Entire frame | Blocks |
> | `_Ledger._dispatch` | Claim/action/accounting | Entire frame, including claim-before-entry intervals | Blocks |
> | `_Ledger._settle` | Settlement mutation | Entire frame | Blocks |
> | `_Ledger._cut` | Cut arbitration | Must have returned before finalization | Blocks |
> | `_RetainingAdapter._retain` | Receipt ingress spanning capture, publication and return | Entire frame, including before `_ingress` | Blocks |
> | `_RetainingAdapter._dequeue` | Separate retained continuation | Entire frame | Blocks; Q also checks retained continuation |
> | `_AcquisitionBridge._enter` | Acquisition admission/composition | Entire frame | Blocks |
> | `_AcquisitionBridge._establish` | Establishment executor | Live executing ancestry blocks; suspended state is checked through Q | Blocks when active |
> | `_AcquisitionBridge._receive` | Bridge receipt ingress | Entire frame | Blocks |
> | `_AcquisitionBridge._finish` | F2 continuation/finish | Entire frame | Blocks; unresolved bridge state independently blocks Q |
> | `_composition_complete` | Composition completion checks | Entire frame | Blocks |
> | Native finalization entry | No separate Python frame | Native record detects nested entry | Provisional or committed reservation rejects reentry |
>
> `_ingress` is currently a **local marker**, not a standalone function. No new function by that name is needed.
>
> Helpers such as `_graph`, `_receiver` and `_composition_owner` do not independently establish receipt. When invoked within an operation above, its enclosing frame supplies exclusion. A diagnostic helper called independently does not become an invented ingress authority.
>
> A suspended generator, retained traceback, pending caller or completed frame is not ACTIVE merely because it remains reachable. Its unresolved semantic obligations still belong in Q/U.
>
> **F before its first marker**
>
> The native scanner compares F’s exact pre-retained code pointer. It does not need F’s locals, signum, receiver or a marker to reject active F.
>
> Public traversal skips incomplete interpreter frames. For the bounded stock interpreter path, that does not justify ignoring an executing pre-marker F:
>
> - F before execution is not yet participating ingress.
> - At the in-profile RESUME/monitoring/pending-check execution boundary, its execution position is publicly traceable.
> - Reentry from a call/monitoring callback before F’s first body marker therefore encounters F’s live code identity.
>
> This must be qualified at that precise boundary. A substituted evaluator or native hook that can re-enter before public frame visibility is **not covered by this proof**; it is not silently admitted by the version check. The source-level basis is the pinned completeness predicate and RESUME sequence. No direct use of those internal fields is proposed. [CPython 3.14.5 frame representation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Include/internal/pycore_interpframe.h), [RESUME implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/bytecodes.c)
>
> **Owner/role authentication**
>
> The scanner does not authenticate an owner by reading frame locals.
>
> A pre-established native record contains:
>
> - Exact SourceAuthority and association identities.
> - Exact source/profile and cut-witness binding.
> - Exact main interpreter/thread binding.
> - Pre-retained canonical code identities.
> - Native writer/reservation state.
> - One-shot finalization state.
> - Retained rejection/admission/result storage.
>
> The canonical association creates and retains this record before source mutation. It cannot be minted from caller-provided booleans or addresses.
>
> Code-pointer comparison answers **“is a disallowed canonical operation active?”** The record answers **“which source authority is requesting finalization?”** These questions remain separate.
>
> No property lookup, descriptor, Python equality/hash, `f_locals` materialization or arbitrary attribute access occurs during native admission.
>
> **Audited public API operation set**
>
> The initial runtime/build gate must already have established compatibility. Admission then permits only this finite inspection set and native scalar/atomic operations:
>
> | Operation | Ownership and behavior | Admission rule |
> |---|---|---|
> | `PyThreadState_Get()` | Borrowed current thread state; requires valid attached context | No thread-state creation |
> | `PyThreadState_GetInterpreter()` | Borrowed interpreter identity | Compare with pre-established identity |
> | `PyThread_get_thread_ident()` | Native thread identifier | Compare with bound main thread |
> | `PyErr_Occurred()` | Borrowed error indicator | Existing error rejects before reservation/inspection |
> | `PyThreadState_GetFrame()` | New frame reference; may materialize; can return NULL without an exception after failure | NULL initial frame always rejects |
> | `PyFrame_GetBack()` | New reference or NULL; may materialize ancestor | NULL plus error rejects; clean NULL ends traversal |
> | `PyFrame_GetCode()` | New code reference; exact frame input | Compare pointer; unexpected failure rejects |
> | `PyErr_GetRaisedException()` | Transfers ownership of an inspection error | Only on terminal rejection path |
> | `PyErr_SetRaisedException()` | Transfers that error back into an empty error indicator | Preserve error without formatting/normalization |
> | `Py_INCREF` / `Py_DECREF` | Restricted to proven anchored frame/code references | IR0 only for scanner cleanup |
> | Pointer/type checks | Exact native identity operations | No rich comparison |
> | Native atomic operations | Reservation/commit/publication | No Python execution |
>
> The pinned frame-materialization implementation explicitly states that it does not execute Python or run GC. It may allocate and produce `MemoryError`; that is not equivalent to success. `PyFrame_GetCode()` uses no allocating lookup in this GIL-enabled path. None of these selected paths intentionally checks signals or releases the GIL. [Frame materialization](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/frame.c), [public frame implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/frameobject.c), [thread-state implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/pystate.c)
>
> Unsupported allocator/evaluator/runtime hooks cannot be assumed callback-free. They remain outside the admitted build/runtime profile.
>
> **Inspection-reference ownership**
>
> Traversal keeps only a bounded number of temporary references at once: current frame, next frame and current code.
>
> | Reference | Independent anchor | Release point | Classification |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | Initial live frame | Executing interpreter frame owns its materialized frame object | After next reference acquired, or rejection | **IR0** |
> | Live back-frame | Its executing ancestor frame owns its frame object | Same traversal discipline | **IR0** |
> | Code returned by `PyFrame_GetCode` | Executing frame retains executable code; canonical entries also have pre-retained identities | Immediately after comparison | **IR0** |
> | Thread/interpreter pointers | Borrowed runtime identities | No DECREF | No discharge obligation |
> | Canonical census code references | Existing function/module ownership plus retained identity record | Not temporary scanner references | Separate identity-record lifetime |
> | Inspection-generated exception | Error indicator → local owned reference → error indicator | Ownership transfer on rejection | No destructive scanner release |
>
> The same thread’s ancestors remain live while the native scan retains the GIL and executes no Python. Scanner releases therefore do not destroy their locals, trace objects or transitive application objects.
>
> A materialized frame may eventually have callback-capable destruction when its actual execution lifetime ends. The scanner does not trigger that destruction through its temporary references.
>
> No IR2/IR3 scanner reference is deferred into an unexplained cleanup queue.
>
> **Error and allocation-failure contract**
>
> - **Existing Python error:** reject immediately, before reservation or frame APIs; preserve it unchanged.
> - **Initial frame acquisition NULL:** reject even if the API cleared its allocation error. Record only “initial ancestry unavailable,” not “no active ingress” or a fabricated OOM diagnosis.
> - **Ancestor materialization failure:** detect the error; transfer it out temporarily, discharge IR0 scanner references, release provisional reservation, then transfer the error back.
> - **Clean end of ancestry:** accept only clean NULL from back traversal after a valid initial frame.
> - **Identity/depth/profile mismatch:** record a fixed native rejection code; do not format a Python error inside the critical section.
> - **Unexpected frame/API result:** reject, never truncate the scan into success.
> - **Depth bound exceeded:** reject the unsupported depth; do not infer the unvisited ancestry is safe.
>
> The initial-frame API’s internal error-clearing path is relevant. With a clear error indicator at entry, its allocation-failure error is the pinned built-in MemoryError path, not an arbitrary pre-existing exception being destroyed. The materializer’s potentially destructive release of a saved exception is avoided by the clear-error precondition. [Pinned exception implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/exceptions.c)
>
> No error formatting, normalization, unraisable hook or general error helper is permitted inside admission.
>
> **Selected ordering and exclusion state machine**
>
> Select **L3: provisional exclusion, inspect, then commit or synchronously reject**.
>
> “OPEN” below means the writer slot is available; it does not mean old receipt authority is open after cut.
>
> | State | Meaning and allowed behavior |
> |---|---|
> | **E0 AVAILABLE** | Bound source owner exists; no finalization reservation. Normal canonical rules apply |
> | **E1 RESERVED** | Native CAS reserves writer exclusion for this attempt; no restoration effects or one-shot finalization commitment yet |
> | **E2 VALIDATED** | Full ancestry scan and A/K/Q/I/U/C/X revalidation succeeded; inspection references discharged |
> | **E3 COMMITTED** | One-shot finalization admission consumed; same native executor owns admitted responsibility |
> | **E4 TERMINAL_SUCCESS** | Restoration/resource results published; ordinary route may resume at protected exit; finalization cannot repeat |
> | **EF TERMINAL_FAILURE** | Admitted partial/uncertain failure retained; no retry/reuse authority |
> | **ER REJECTED** | Precommit rejection recorded; reservation returned to E0 before Python resumes |
> | **SC PROCESS_FAILURE** | Crash/corruption; no in-process publication/release guarantee |
>
> Transitions:
>
> 1. **E0→E1:** compare-and-exchange, acquire/release semantics, exact owner/attempt identity. Losing contender rejects without changing the winner.
> 2. **E1→E2:** scan complete live ancestry; reject census matches; revalidate semantic record; discharge scanner references. No source effects.
> 3. **E2→E3:** release-published commit transition. This is the **finalization admission linearization point**.
> 4. **E1/E2→ER→E0:** fixed rejection record, IR0 cleanup, release-store reservation removal, then return/error propagation.
> 5. **E3→E4/EF:** same native invocation publishes terminal results and transitions the record. It never resets one-shot admission.
> 6. **Any state→SC:** crash/abort/corruption has no implied successful cleanup.
>
> A precommit rejection does not consume finalization admission. A later otherwise-lawful request is not replay of a restoration effect that never began. This does not permit resuming a FAILED invocation.
>
> After E3, a returned error cannot restore E0. EF is deliberately terminal, not an accidentally forgotten lock.
>
> **Why I is not stale**
>
> Between the scan and E3:
>
> - The main-thread native call retains the GIL.
> - It performs no Python callback or signal check.
> - Native configuration writers are excluded by E1.
> - Native signal recording remains allowed.
> - No admitted cross-thread participating-retention producer exists outside the canonical ingress contract.
> - Any pre-existing main-thread ingress frame is an ancestor and causes rejection.
> - A Python caller waiting for execution has not thereby become participating ingress.
>
> Thus the absence observed by the complete scan remains true until commit. E3 binds I to the exact owner, attempt, cut and census identities.
>
> This is not a general claim that the GIL serializes all native activity.
>
> **Thread and producer boundary**
>
> | Participant | Concurrent with scan? | Treatment |
> |---|---|---|
> | Main-thread F/receipt/retention already active | Native attempt can be nested beneath it | Appears in ancestry; reject |
> | Another Python thread waiting for GIL | Cannot run Python during scan | Pending attempt, not active participation |
> | Cross-thread canonical ingress call | Must obey the current main-thread/profile gate | Cannot become hidden participating retention |
> | Native signal producer | Yes | May record pending delivery; does not need writer token |
> | External signal sender | Yes | Same; generation is not participating retention |
> | Native configuration writer | Yes in principle | Must share the same reservation protocol; losing writer performs no effect |
> | Native participating retainer | Not supplied by this bounded contract | Adding one requires an integrated ingress protocol; cannot be treated as a mere producer |
>
> An external native queue already retaining on behalf of the owner would invalidate this topology. The scan cannot prove that such a queue is empty or absent.
>
> **Ordering alternatives**
>
> - **L1, inspect first:** can work only with a complete no-callback/no-native-writer gap proof and revalidation; offers no advantage here.
> - **L2, acquire irrevocable admission before inspection:** rejected, because an active ancestor would be discovered after consuming finalization authority.
> - **L3, provisional reservation:** selected; permits callback-free clean rejection before one-shot commit.
> - No Python `finally` or later executor participates in release.
>
> **Race proof**
>
> | Race | Resolution |
> |---|---|
> | A. F pre-marker ↔ admission | Live code match rejects; marker is unnecessary |
> | B. Receipt begins ↔ reservation | Existing main-thread frame rejects; new Python execution cannot start during scan |
> | C. Retention begins ↔ admission | Same, plus Q independently detects retained unresolved continuation |
> | D. Post-cut explicit ingress ↔ admission | Canonical old-ingress rejection remains; no fabricated O delivery |
> | E. Pending signal recording ↔ admission | Recording remains allowed; no membership inferred from pending alone |
> | F. Native writer ↔ finalizer | Reservation CAS orders them; loser performs no configuration effect |
> | G. Same-owner reentry | Existing reservation rejects; ancestor census independently catches canonical nested entry |
> | H. Scan succeeds ↔ new ingress attempt | GIL/no-callback interval plus reservation prevents participation before E3 |
> | I. Reservation held ↔ active ancestor found | IR0 cleanup and E1→E0 release occur before returning |
> | J. Rejection ↔ caller resumes | Native reservation is already released; caller continues only under existing cut/receipt rules |
>
> No rejected attempt clears membership, claim, pending state or the cut.
>
> **Future qualification additions**
>
> Common oracle: fixed isolated topology; exact artifact/runtime identities; positive target witness; candidate admission/result evidence captured before ambient cleanup. A missing intended boundary is **INCONCLUSIVE**, not success.
>
> | History | Required witness and outcome |
> |---|---|
> | F pre-marker | Trace/monitor boundary before first body marker, with F visible in ancestry; finalization rejects |
> | F after marker | Exact F frame and marker; same rejection |
> | Receipt/classification active | Canonical frame and original membership state; rejection leaves it unchanged |
> | Retention active | Retention frame/capture boundary; no finalization admission or membership shift |
> | Dequeue/finish active | Exact continuation identity; rejection preserves one-shot continuation |
> | No active ingress | Complete scan, valid semantic record and E3 witness; no skipped ancestors |
> | Same-owner reentry | E1/E3 identity already present; second attempt cannot change it |
> | New Python ingress after reservation | Attempt blocks on actual execution; after return it obeys cut rules |
> | Clean rejection after reservation | E1 observed, rejection cause observed, E0 published before caller resumes |
> | Native pending recording during scan | Native generation/recording witness; no Python callback or pending clearing inside scan |
> | Native writer contention | Shared-token contention; losing writer produces zero effects |
> | Initial-frame allocation failure | NULL initial acquisition leads to rejection, never empty-ancestry admission |
> | Ancestor allocation failure | Error preserved, reservation released and all scanner references accounted |
> | Code/frame mismatch | No partial scan accepted |
> | Existing error state | Exact original error retained; no reservation, formatting or replacement |
> | Inspection cleanup | Refcount/ownership instrumentation proves no scanner-induced frame/code destruction |
> | Failure after E3 | EF/result retained; no E0 reset, replay or cleanup-as-success |
> | Native crash | Parent records only independently observed evidence; no assumed release/publication |
>
> Pre-marker instrumentation establishes a reentry stress schedule, not proof that production instrumentation exists. Native producer tests must not claim that a Python producer ran while another thread retained the GIL.
>
> **Exact admission contract**
>
> > After safe runtime/profile authentication, the existing source owner may reserve the native writer slot provisionally. With the GIL retained and no Python execution, the admission operation traverses the current live ancestry through the audited public APIs, rejects every canonical census-code match, validates the pre-established association and semantic facts, and discharges all inspection references. Only then may it atomically publish one-shot finalization admission. Precommit rejection synchronously releases the provisional reservation before Python resumes. Postcommit failure retains admitted responsibility and never restores eligibility. Runtime-pending signal recording remains permitted throughout. No frame-local inspection, internal-frame access, pending-state mutation or Python-finally release is used.
>
> **Effect on the six findings**
>
> - **BLOCKING 1:** R0 remains a prospective correction; scanner references now have their separate ownership contract.
> - **BLOCKING 2:** R1 gains an operational I witness.
> - **BLOCKING 3:** publication can bind to E3’s owner/attempt/census/semantic identities.
> - **MAJOR 4:** this active-ingress admission boundary is now a **CORRECTED-CANDIDATE input**.
> - **MAJOR 5:** incorporate the admission oracles above.
> - **MAJOR 6:** bind the public API paths, stock evaluator/GIL profile and code census into runtime/build qualification.
>

> ## 5. B2.1 selected representation
>
> **Select option B: an explicitly owned native anchor, separate from the Python collectible graph.**
>
> This candidate admits **one published source-lifetime binding per closed process profile**. This restriction applies to prospective CS-R1, not existing I1–I4 APIs. The Registry may already contain other associations; CS-R1 still validates current membership and selected-work identity.
>
> The representation consists of:
>
> 1. **Process-lifetime native profile anchor.**
>    - Created during S3/Phase-A bootstrap.
>    - Owns the metadata bundle.
>    - Owns one native cell lease from successful binding construction until successful extra-root release.
>    - Does not select work, grant admission or perform cleanup.
>
> 2. **Native cell.**
>    - Ordinary native allocation, not a Python object.
>    - Holds exactly one owning Registry reference while root-held.
>    - Holds immutable lifetime/profile/table identities, native control state and bounded evidence.
>    - Stores Association/work identities only for comparison after current root traversal.
>
> 3. **Exact Python-visible SourceAuthority handle.**
>    - One immutable native heap-type instance per cell.
>    - Contains a native cell pointer and owns one native cell lease.
>    - Contains no Python root, Association, work or metadata reference.
>    - Is retained canonically by `Association._source_lifetime`.
>
> Alternative assessment:
>
> | Option | Result |
> |---|---|
> | A: fully traversed GC cell alone | Insufficient: an otherwise unreachable cycle can be collected while responsibility remains unresolved. |
> | B: independently owned native anchor | Selected; root ownership exists independently of collection of Python cycles. |
> | C: Python owner plus separate token | Can express similar ownership, but adds another handle/token relationship without reducing the required anchor proof. |
> | D: smaller representation | No smaller representation was established that preserves caller-loss retention and ordinary successful release. |
>
> This is reference ownership, not new K/Q/U authority.
>
> ## 6. B2.1 GC contract
>
> The SourceAuthority handle is a non-GC native heap-type instance:
>
> - Direct base: `object`.
> - No instance dictionary, weak references, subclassing or Python finalizer.
> - No caller-accessible construction.
> - No Python-reference fields beyond its ordinary heap-type reference.
> - No `Py_TPFLAGS_HAVE_GC`, `tp_traverse` or `tp_clear`.
> - Fixed native allocation/deallocation functions.
> - Deallocation drops its native lease, frees handle memory and releases its heap-type reference.
>
> The metadata anchor independently retains the handle type, so that type decrement is non-last during supported execution.
>
> The Registry reference is **not an omitted reference owned by the handle**. It belongs to the separately anchored native cell. Handle deallocation cannot release it.
>
> Current Registry/Association/Invocation containers retain their existing CPython GC behavior. The external owning Registry reference prevents their reachable responsibility graph from becoming an isolated collectible cycle. After successful root release, ordinary GC may collect that graph when its other owners disappear.
>
> This distinction follows CPython’s requirement that extension containers account for their own Python references; it does not justify concealing an ordinary container-owned edge. [CPython 3.14.5 GC contract](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Doc/c-api/gcsupport.rst)
>
> ## 7. B2.1 metadata lifetime
>
> The native profile anchor owns a finite, immutable bundle containing:
>
> - Expected-schema identity and complete S3 table.
> - Loaded project/native type identities.
> - Required descriptor/member identities.
> - Expected class-dictionary entries.
> - Required function/code/default/keyword-default/closure identities.
> - Required singleton and enum identities.
> - Exact associated module objects and native artifact identity.
> - Profile eligibility latch.
>
> References are acquired before canonical responsibility. Each retained object has an explicit owning entry; identity deduplication does not remove required ownership.
>
> The bundle is retained for the supported process lifetime—**ER2**, not speculative teardown. Successful cell completion does not destroy it. A failed bootstrap after acquiring references permanently makes that bootstrap ineligible and retains its bounded partial bundle until process termination.
>
> Consequences:
>
> - Removing a module name from `sys.modules` cannot destroy the owned module.
> - Module/type names are never ownership evidence.
> - Cell destruction requires no metadata decrements.
> - P1 evidence readers need only native scalar data.
> - No reseal, second bootstrap or module-unload/reinitialization cycle is admitted.
>
> Interpreter finalization remains outside the valid Python-object access interval.
>
> ## 8. B2.1 unresolved-failure retention
>
> After BF:
>
> - The anchor retains its native cell lease.
> - The cell retains the extra Registry reference.
> - The Registry retains its existing association graph.
> - Association retains SourceAuthority; SourceAuthority retains its native lease.
> - The metadata bundle remains retained.
> - New root-capable entry is permanently forbidden.
> - Evidence-only reading remains permitted.
> - Binding replacement, new source lifetime and source-work reuse are forbidden.
>
> Evidence storage is fixed-size: one immutable E2/result area plus a bounded atomic diagnostic record. No list grows per rejection or reader operation. Attempt counters cannot wrap into reusable identity; exhaustion rejects further entry.
>
> The retained graph includes everything already owned by the Registry, potentially more than one Association. The finite claim therefore requires the closed-profile prohibition on new work and replacement lifetimes. It is **finite retained responsibility**, not successful cleanup or leak-free operation.
>
> ## 9. B2.1 successful release / ER classification
>
> The additional Registry decrement is **ER0**, conditional on a current proof immediately before it:
>
> 1. Exact root and exact association map.
> 2. Selected work still maps to selected Association.
> 3. Selected Association’s `registry` slot still strongly owns the exact root.
> 4. No other root-capable operation exists.
> 5. No Python, GC, teardown or canonical writer can intervene.
> 6. All borrowed descendant uses finish before clearing the native root slot.
>
> Then:
>
> - Clear the native root slot once.
> - Decrement the former owning reference once.
> - Publish root-released state.
> - Drop the anchor’s cell lease.
>
> The Association-owned reference makes the decrement non-last. Instrumentation contributes no reference to that proof.
>
> A failed release proof after E3 produces retained failure, not precommit rejection or speculative decrement.
>
> ## 10. B2.1 shutdown boundary
>
> Supported success ends before interpreter finalization begins.
>
> Unsupported:
>
> - `Py_Finalize`/reinitialization while a cell may operate.
> - Subinterpreter teardown/reuse.
> - Extension unloading.
> - Python-object access after interpreter finalization starts.
>
> At process/interpreter shutdown with unresolved responsibility, retained native references are not decremented as a cleanup strategy. Process reclamation establishes neither restoration nor resource completion.
>
> Handle/native-cell destructors perform no source restoration, receipt, cut, repair or COMPLETE transition.
>

> ## 12. B2.2 state machine
>
> | State | Representation | Legal exits |
> |---|---|---|
> | B0 UNBOUND | Association slot is `None`; no published cell | B1 |
> | B1 CONSTRUCTING | Native-local candidate and construction lease; not externally accessible | B0 on clean failure; B2 on publication; SC |
> | B2 PUBLISHED | Canonical slot owns initialized handle; native cell active/root-held | Precommit rejection remains B2; B3; permanent ineligibility without admission; SC |
> | B3 ADMITTED | E3 committed; original finalizer owns P0 | Closing; BF; SC |
> | Closing | New P0 permanently closed; original finalizer completes release proof | B4; BF; SC |
> | B4 ROOT RELEASED | Root NULL; P0 permanently closed; evidence retained | Ordinary native destruction after leases end |
> | BF UNRESOLVED | Root/anchor retained; P0 permanently closed | Evidence reads; SC/process termination |
> | SC | Execution/lifetime proof lost | No inferred clean transition |
>
> Profile invalidation before E3 does not manufacture BF or invocation FAILED. It makes further semantic admission ineligible while preserving owned state.

> ## 13. B2.2 publication linearization
>
> The operation runs under the admitted main-thread GIL without Python execution, signal processing or GIL release during its indivisible portion.
>
> Before publication:
>
> - Native cell and handle are allocated.
> - Root ownership is acquired.
> - All immutable identities, counters, evidence and native leases are initialized.
> - Anchor ownership is established.
> - The Association slot remains `None`.
>
> **Publication linearizes at the canonical assignment of the initialized handle to the authenticated `_source_lifetime` member.**
>
> The selected writer uses the exact native member-assignment path through `PyObject_GenericSetAttr`, restricted to that authenticated member. It is not an E1→E2 helper and cannot be used for canonical reads or arbitrary assignments. With the exact member descriptor, ready type and previous `None`, the admitted setter path performs reference transfer without Python reentry. [Pinned assignment implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/object.c), [member setter](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/descrobject.c)
>
> No externally callable handle exists before this store. Source installation must reauthenticate the resulting canonical edge.
>
> ## 14. Reentrant/repeated binding
>
> | Case | Frozen result |
> |---|---|
> | First legitimate bind | Publish and return the exact handle. |
> | Reentry before native operation begins | Whichever native operation first completes publication wins; later call rejects. |
> | Reentry during native construction | Impossible in the admitted callback-free construction path. |
> | Other Python thread during construction | Cannot enter the GIL-held native operation. |
> | Native/non-main-thread binding attempt | Clean context rejection before construction. |
> | Any repeated bind after publication | Clean rejection; never retrieval or replacement. |
> | Foreign Registry, stale Association, wrong work/Association | Clean rejection before root acquisition. |
> | Wrong profile/lifetime | Clean rejection. |
> | Bind after installation, BF or B4 | Clean rejection. |
>
> Obtaining an existing binding for an otherwise authorized operation means traversing the canonical edge; it does not mean calling bind again.
>
> ## 15. Unpublished/losing disposal
>
> A contender that finds an existing binding rejects **before allocation**. It owns no candidate root or wrapper.
>
> For construction failure:
>
> 1. Candidate is still private and has no source effects.
> 2. Finish all borrowed uses.
> 3. Clear/decrement any candidate root reference while the binding call’s independently live Registry argument still owns it: ER0.
> 4. Remove the unpublished anchor lease.
> 5. Release the private handle.
> 6. Drop the construction lease and free native storage.
>
> A private handle’s last decrement is **ER1**: its exact native destructor has no weakref, Python finalizer, arbitrary Python-owned fields or source cleanup. Its heap-type release is ER0.
>
> Metadata is shared anchor-owned ER2 storage; no losing object releases it.
>
> ## 16. Interruption-before-publication table
>
> | Boundary | Authority/visibility | Required outcome |
> |---|---|---|
> | Before root acquisition | B0; no candidate authority | Clean rejection; later separately initiated bind may occur. |
> | After native allocation | Private B1 | Free native candidate. |
> | After root acquisition | Private B1, independently anchored | ER0 root discharge, then native disposal. |
> | After metadata binding | Private B1 | Drop borrowed bundle pointer; bundle remains ER2. |
> | After handle allocation | Private B1 | Root discharge, ER1 private-handle destruction, native disposal. |
> | Immediately before publication | Still unbound | Same clean disposal if a native failure occurs. |
> | Immediately after publication | B2 is authoritative | Retain binding; never treat it as unpublished. |
> | Process crash at any boundary | SC | No cleanup/publication success inferred. |
>
> Python exceptions/signals do not interrupt the indivisible native portion. A Python exception after return can lose the return value, but cannot remove the published binding. None of these binding failures manufactures invocation FAILED.
>

>

> ## 18. Live-handle model
>
> Select **model A**.
>
> Every operation starts from an independently live exact SourceAuthority handle passed through the ordinary Python/C call boundary. Its native lease keeps the cell alive before pin acquisition.
>
> No API accepts a raw address, cell ID or E2 record as a substitute.
>
> A native evidence worker may receive an already-acquired P1 pin before it starts. That pin transfer carries native storage lifetime only; it carries no Python object access.
>
> A stale but live handle can safely reach the cell and reject based on terminal native state.
>
> ## 19. Pin taxonomy
>
> Here **P0/P1-pin** are native pin names; **P1-ancestry** denotes the preserved public-frame contract.
>
> | Pin | Acquisition | Rights | Terminal behavior |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | P0 root-capable | Live handle; main-thread GIL; eligible B2; no existing P0 | Root traversal, semantic validation, E2/E3 and final release proof | No new acquisition after E3, closing, BF or B4 |
> | P1 evidence-only | Live handle or previously transferred live pin | Acquire-read committed native scalar evidence only | Allowed through B3/closing/BF/B4 |
> | Construction lease | Private native constructor only | Prevent unpublished cell destruction | Ends before returning a published handle or after disposal |
>
> Only one P0 exists. P1 count has a checked finite maximum; overflow rejects rather than wrapping. Construction ownership is not a third operational capability.
>
> ## 20. Pin/destruction protocol
>
> One lock-free native control word contains phase, lease-presence flags, P0 occupancy and the checked P1 count. Its representation and allowed combinations are manifest-bound.
>
> Acquisition/decrement uses compare-exchange with acquire/release ordering. A live handle lease exists before touching the word.
>
> Destruction is possible only after:
>
> - Anchor lease absent.
> - Handle lease absent.
> - P0 absent.
> - P1 count zero.
> - Root NULL.
> - State permits ordinary destruction.
>
> The last lease transition exclusively claims destruction. No second caller can acquire from freed storage because all acquisition routes require an already-live lease.
>
> Native destruction frees native storage only. A root-held last-lease state is an invariant failure, not permission to decrement the root in a destructor.
>
> ## 21. Permanent read closure
>
> The successful finalizer changes B3 to **Closing** after resource terminal publication.
>
> This transition:
>
> - Permanently forbids new P0.
> - Preserves the finalizer’s existing P0.
> - Leaves P1 readers unaffected.
> - Does not mean source CLOSED, invocation COMPLETE or object destruction.
>
> A contender cannot reopen B2. It rejects from native state without reading the root.
>
> ## 22. Root release with pins
>
> Frozen order:
>
> 1. Existing enclosing operation publishes verified restoration.
> 2. R0 callable references are discharged under R0.
> 3. Resource terminal result is published.
> 4. Finalizer commits permanent P0 closure.
> 5. Confirm its P0 is the sole P0.
> 6. Perform final current-edge ER0 proof.
> 7. End borrowed descendant use.
> 8. Clear root slot.
> 9. Perform exactly one ER0 root decrement.
> 10. Publish B4/root-released.
> 11. Drop anchor lease.
> 12. Release finalizer P0.
> 13. Permit protected exit.
>
> The finalizer remains counted as the sole P0 until step 12; no special uncounted reader exists. P1 readers never delay root release, but can delay native storage destruction.
>
> ## 23. BF behavior
>
> The admitted executor records bounded failure evidence, ends borrowed uses and commits BF with P0 entry permanently closed.
>
> Then it releases its P0 while:
>
> - Anchor/root/metadata remain retained.
> - No new finalization attempt is admitted.
> - P1 evidence reading remains possible.
> - No diagnostic root traversal is added.
> - No cleanup/recovery executor is created.
>
> BF does not reset the E3 admission or make source configuration healthy.
>
> ## 24. Successful post-root-release state
>
> Association may continue owning SourceAuthority; SourceAuthority continues owning a rootless cell.
>
> Remaining cell data is native:
>
> - Lifetime/attempt identity.
> - E2/E3 and enclosing result witnesses.
> - Terminal phase.
> - P1 reader accounting.
> - Borrowed pointer to process-lifetime metadata, never used by P1 to dereference Python objects.
>
> Root is NULL and P0 is permanently closed. Later handle/cell destruction performs no semantic cleanup.
>
> Source handoff to O is an enclosing R0/R1 prerequisite of this release sequence, not a result inferred from B4.
>
> ## 25. H1 complete whitelist
>
> **A complete whole-E1→E2 whitelist is not established.** The following is the exact proposed whitelist for CS-R1’s canonical-validation portion.
>
> Common conditions for every admitted row:
>
> - Independently live input.
> - Exact precondition checked before operation.
> - GIL held; stable current owners; no teardown.
> - No Python callback, descriptor invocation, hashing/equality dispatch, coercion, signal check, pending-call processing or GIL release.
> - No allocation/GC in the admitted path.
> - Borrowed results remain within their owner’s protected lifetime.
> - Unexpected failures stop validation; no diagnostic formatting occurs.
>
> | Exact operation | Purpose/precondition | Classification; ownership | Path |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | `Py_TYPE` and C pointer equality | Exact live-object category and identity | Public full C API; borrowed/scalar | Validation |
> | `PyDict_Size` | Exact dict cardinality | Public/Stable API; scalar | Validation |
> | `PyDict_Next` | Exact stable dict entries; local position starts at zero | Public/Stable API; borrowed key/value | Validation |
> | `PyTuple_GET_SIZE` | Exact tuple length | Public full-API macro; scalar | Validation |
> | `PyTuple_GET_ITEM` | Exact tuple, proven bounds | Public full-API macro; borrowed item | Validation |
> | `PyUnicode_GET_LENGTH` | Exact Unicode length | Public full-API representation accessor; scalar | Validation |
> | `PyUnicode_KIND`, `PyUnicode_DATA`, `PyUnicode_READ` | Exact Unicode; valid kind; checked index | Public full-API representation accessors; borrowed buffer/scalar | Validation |
> | `PyLong_AsLongAndOverflow` | Exact `PyLong_Type` before call | Public API; scalar; no coercion branch admitted | Validation |
> | `PyType_GetDict` | Retained, ready, exact authenticated type | Public API; new dict reference | Validation |
> | `PyGen_GetCode` | Exact live generator | Public full API; new code reference | Validation |
> | `PyFunction_GetCode` | Exact authenticated function | Public API; borrowed required code | Validation |
> | `PyFunction_GetDefaults` | Exact authenticated function | Public API; borrowed or NULL-as-absent | Validation |
> | `PyFunction_GetKwDefaults` | Same | Public API; borrowed or NULL-as-absent | Validation |
> | `PyFunction_GetClosure` | Same | Public API; borrowed or NULL-as-absent | Validation |
> | `PyErr_Occurred` | Attached admitted thread state | Public API; borrowed observation only | Entry/error check |
> | `Py_DECREF` | Only the explicitly proved ER0 temporaries | Public macro; non-last | Validation/rejection |
> | Fixed-size `memcpy` | Authenticated object-reference slot | C operation; borrowed pointer result | Validation |
> | Checked integer arithmetic, branch, fixed-array access | Native lengths, masks and indices | C scalar operations | Both |
> | `atomic_load_explicit`, `atomic_store_explicit`, `atomic_compare_exchange_strong_explicit` | Manifest-bound lock-free native state | C11 atomics; no Python ownership | Both |
> | Fixed native rejection/evidence stores | Preallocated storage only | Native | Both |
>
> No generic attribute getter, Python iterator, dictionary lookup, UTF-8 conversion or arbitrary sequence/numeric API is admitted.
>
> The pinned implementations support the stated dictionary, type-dictionary and function-getter ownership distinctions. [Dictionary implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/dictobject.c), [type dictionary getter](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/typeobject.c), [function getters](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/funcobject.c)
>
> **Open boundary:** P1-ancestry’s exact helpers are not added speculatively. Public frame materialization can allocate; `PyThreadState_GetFrame` also clears an error on failed materialization. The checkpoint does not establish whether those operations occur inside the interval now required to be allocation-free. [Pinned thread-state accessor](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/pystate.c), [frame materialization](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/frame.c)
>
> ## 26. Exact string/integer validation
>
> | Family | Selected method |
> |---|---|
> | Registry work keys during runtime traversal | Exact Unicode, nonzero length; current Association.work and selected bound work compared by pointer identity |
> | Work supplied to binding | Exact Unicode; nonallocating code-point comparison against current canonical map keys |
> | Fixed preparation/composition labels | Exact Unicode; length plus canonical code-point comparison against fixed native labels |
> | Class-image dictionary names | Same nonallocating comparison against retained expected names; corresponding values by identity |
> | Integer cell keys | Exact `PyLong_Type`, then `PyLong_AsLongAndOverflow`; accept only result zero with no overflow |
> | `None`, booleans, `_CUT`, profile/outcome singletons | Exact retained pointer identity |
>
> No interning assumption is required for equal string labels. Exact integers exclude bool and subclasses before conversion; the pinned exact-long branch avoids `__index__` and owning temporaries. [Unicode accessors](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Include/cpython/unicodeobject.h), [integer conversion](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/longobject.c)
>
> Finite container contracts:
>
> | Container | Representation |
> |---|---|
> | Registry map | Exact dict; nonempty exact-string keys; exact Associations with reverse identities |
> | Fault dictionaries | Exact dict; nonempty means rejection; no formatting of contents |
> | First/claim/entry cells | Exact dict; at most key zero; exact object-token value |
> | Preparation | Exact dict; only zero-token and `"configuration"` tuple entries |
> | Configuration | Exact tuple of length two; current facts and binding by identity |
> | Composition | Exact dict; only `"adapter"` and `"acquisition"` with exact role types |
> | Adapter attempt/dequeue | Exact bounded zero-key object-token cells |
> | Bridge errors | Exact dict; success requires empty |
> | Class-image dictionaries | Exact size and complete expected name/value matching; no order dependency |
>
> ## 27. Raw slot-load primitive
>
> Select a **fixed-size byte copy into a local `PyObject *` representation**.
>
> Before loading:
>
> 1. Containing object is live independently of this load.
> 2. Exact role/type is authenticated.
> 3. A compiled internal role selects the immutable S3 entry.
> 4. Defining class/member/object-reference representation match.
> 5. Offset, object extent and alignment were checked at bootstrap.
> 6. Runtime arithmetic verifies the offset and pointer-size extent without overflow.
>
> The helper copies exactly `sizeof(PyObject *)` bytes from the authenticated slot representation into the local pointer object. This avoids dereferencing a fabricated pointer-to-pointer lvalue with unresolved aliasing assumptions.
>
> NULL is a native rejection. Non-NULL is borrowed; nested access requires its own representation/type check.
>
> The admitted Darwin arm64 build must establish pointer width, object layout, compiler behavior and actual load expansion. No atomic slot load is required when GIL, root lifetime and writer exclusion are genuine. Atomicity cannot repair any missing premise.
>
> ## 28. Python-error policy
>
> For the canonical-validation portion:
>
> | Situation | Required behavior |
> |---|---|
> | Error already set | Reject before semantic loads; leave error untouched |
> | Helper unexpectedly sets error | Stop immediately; preserve indicator; native reason records helper failure |
> | Semantic rejection without Python error | Record native reason; construct Python diagnostic only after protected release |
> | Temporary cleanup | ER0 releases only; no fetch/normalize/format/clear |
> | Returning to Python | Preserve existing error; otherwise create a fixed diagnostic after E1 release |
>
> An impossible representation/native corruption is SC, not a promise that an API’s arbitrary error path is safe.
>
> This policy cannot yet be claimed for all of E1→E2 because the preserved P1 helper/error behavior and ordering remain unspecified in the accessible fixed contract.
>
> ## 29. Temporary-reference inventory
>
> | Reference | Owner/lifetime | Release class |
> |---|---|---|
> | Registry root | Anchored cell; entire root-held lifetime | Successful ER0; unresolved retention |
> | Current slot results | Current strong parent edges | Borrowed; no decrement |
> | Dict keys/values | Stable exact dict | Borrowed |
> | Tuple elements | Stable exact tuple | Borrowed |
> | Unicode data | Live immutable exact string | Borrowed |
> | Function getter results | Retained function | Borrowed; optional NULL distinguished |
> | `PyGen_GetCode` result | Temporary plus live generator’s executable ownership | ER0 |
> | `PyType_GetDict` result | Temporary plus live retained type ownership | ER0 |
> | Metadata bundle references | Explicit process anchor | ER2 |
> | Existing Python error | Interpreter-owned; only observed | No acquired reference |
> | Private unpublished handle | Native constructor | ER1 destructor; its type release ER0 |
> | Published handle during construction return | Canonical Association independently owns it | ER0 for a discarded local reference |
> | P1-ancestry references | Preserved separate IR0 contract | Exact inventory/order still required |
>
> `PyType_GetDict` is deliberately included during runtime class-image validation as well as bootstrap; eliminating it would require a separate equivalent image proof.
>
> `PyGen_GetCode` increments the returned code reference in the pinned implementation. [Generator implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/genobject.c)
>
> No descendant convenience INCREF is introduced.
>
> ## 30. Callback-free bounded claim
>
> For the listed canonical-validation operations, under their exact preconditions, the proposed interval performs no Python execution, allocation, GC, signal processing, GIL release or callback-capable last decrement.
>
> **This is not yet a claim about the complete E1→E2 interval.**
>
> The smallest missing fact is:
>
> > What exact P1-ancestry operations and reference discharges occur after E1 acquisition, and does the frozen contract already place potentially allocating public-frame materialization outside the interval required here to be allocation-free?
>
> Moving P1, pre-materializing frames or weakening “allocation-free” would be architectural choices; none is silently selected.
>
> ## 31. MX setup classes
>
> Use exactly:
>
> - **C:** canonical history.
> - **D:** safe non-authoritative decoy.
> - **N:** separately authenticated negative artifact.
> - **I:** fixed native instrumentation.
> - **O:** external/parent observation.
> - **Q:** previously permitted Q2 pre-E3 instrumentation only.
>
> D/N/Q never manufacture canonical K/Q/U. Instrumented native evidence contains scalars and IDs, not extra candidate Python owners.
>
> ## 32. Target-acknowledgement contract
>
> Each history records:
>
> - Artifact/profile/run identity.
> - Intended gate ID.
> - Attempt/lifetime ID.
> - Target-reached marker.
> - Current-edge attribution where relevant.
> - Candidate disposition.
> - Protected interval boundaries.
> - Cleanup-start marker.
>
> Target and disposition are separate. Missing target is INCONCLUSIVE. A forbidden effect can independently establish FAIL even when a normal target marker is absent.
>
> E2/E3 markers come from their actual commit operations, never fixture assignments.
>
> ## 33. Internal-gate decoy rule
>
> A decoy may test only the gate it actually reaches.
>
> For internal comparisons, a qualification-only helper harness may supply a non-authoritative comparison operand **after canonical traversal has identified the real current object**. Its claim is limited to that comparison helper.
>
> It does not prove that an end-to-end candidate can acquire a stale canonical graph.
>
> Impossible current states are covered by source/invariant proof or safely rejected negative artifacts. They are not manufactured by editing canonical dictionaries.
>
> ## 34. Writer-attempt oracles
>
> Three different producer protocols are required:
>
> | Producer | Positive evidence |
> |---|---|
> | Python writer | Native precursor marks “about to acquire GIL”; handshake establishes validation active; effect marker cannot precede protected exit |
> | Native configuration writer | Exact reservation-acquisition attempt and failed/blocked result while candidate owns E1; zero source effect |
> | Runtime signal producer | Actual permitted signal generation and native recording; no Python signal processing inside protected interval |
>
> A Python thread holding the GIL does not let another Python thread execute a Python “attempt” marker inside the interval. That attempt must be acknowledged before GIL acquisition in the native producer path.
>
> ## 35. Pin/destruction oracles
>
> A live handle must exist before a pin attempt.
>
> Required native markers are:
>
> `HANDLE_LIVE → PIN_ATTEMPT → PIN_RESULT → USE_BEGIN/NO_USE → PIN_RELEASE`
>
> Destruction has:
>
> `LAST_LEASE_ATTEMPT → DESTROY_CLAIMED → FREE`
>
> The impossible combination “object destroyed while a valid handle still owns its lease” is an invariant violation. A fixture must not create a dangling pointer to simulate a legitimate acquisition.
>
> Native barriers establish P1 overlap and contender timing. A P0 contender can test busy/closed rejection; it cannot create a second valid P0.
>
> ## 36. Root-release/ER0 oracle
>
> The release operation emits preallocated scalar acknowledgements at:
>
> 1. Current map selected exact Association.
> 2. Reverse Registry edge matched.
> 3. New P0 closed.
> 4. Sole finalizer P0 confirmed.
> 5. Root held.
> 6. Borrowed uses ended.
> 7. Root slot cleared.
> 8. Root decrement executed.
> 9. Root-released publication completed.
>
> The instrumentation records identities without owning references. Its source audit must prove no observer INCREF, GC edge or hidden wrapper-to-Registry reference.
>
> Survival alone is not the oracle. The oracle combines independently audited strong-edge semantics with actual current-edge and decrement acknowledgements.
>
> A helper-only release test does not qualify enclosing restoration/resource publication.
>
> ## 37. Callback-free interval oracle
>
> Two kinds of evidence are required:
>
> - **Source/artifact proof:** closed call graph, permitted operations, reference releases, generated native code and instrumentation equivalence.
> - **Execution evidence:** actual protected entry, operation/exit markers, native callback/GIL/GC/pending-path observations appropriate to the exact artifact.
>
> An experiment cannot prove a universal negative by observing one quiet run.
>
> The complete interval oracle remains pending the P1 ordering question. No “no allocation” PASS may exclude P1 operations while labelling itself whole-E1→E2.
>
> ## 38. Artifact-equivalence contract
>
> Transfer requires identical relevant:
>
> - Project source and schema.
> - Native cell representation and ownership.
> - GC/type/deallocation behavior.
> - Binding/pin/closure protocol.
> - Raw-load body.
> - Helper/error/reference behavior.
> - Memory ordering.
> - E2/E3 commit logic.
> - Source effects.
>
> Permitted differences are fixed scalar observation storage and native barriers. Barriers must not release the GIL, acquire Python references or replace reservation operations.
>
> Helper harnesses, negative artifacts and Q2 histories retain narrower claims. Their results do not automatically qualify production success.
>
> ## 39. Complete corrected qualification matrix
>
> **The whole-target matrix is not closed**, because the complete P1-inclusive interval target cannot yet be specified faithfully. The following is the concrete prospective matrix for the resolved mechanisms and identified helper boundaries.
>
> Common row contract:
>
> - **Topology:** isolated candidate process; one canonical binding; parent observer. Additional native/Python actors are named below.
> - **Deadline:** 12 seconds.
> - **Synchronization:** explicit native handshake/barrier, never sleep-only.
> - **PASS:** target reached, expected disposition and attributed evidence.
> - **FAIL:** contradictory disposition or independently observed forbidden effect.
> - **INCONCLUSIVE:** absent target, invalid fixture, missing attribution or timeout without decisive evidence.
> - **Cleanup:** capture and commit candidate evidence first; cleanup cannot upgrade it.
> - **No execution is authorized by this matrix.**
>
> Every row inherits these rules.
>
> | ID | Setup | Exact precondition / actor / synchronization | Positive target and candidate evidence | Expected disposition / bounded limitation |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | A1 | C+O | Published binding; initiating references dropped before collection | Anchor/root-held and canonical binding identities | Retained; tests caller-loss ownership |
> | A2 | C+I+O | A1; ordinary GC before E1 | GC completed, same binding/root available at later valid entry | Survives; no GC during validation |
> | A3 | C+I+O | Canonically produced BF; caller loss then GC | BF/root-held, same native cell | Retained; no successful cleanup claim |
> | A4 | C+I | Successful enclosing release, then ordinary owner loss/GC | B4, handle release and last native lease | Native destruction without source effects |
> | A5 | C+D+I | Module name removed through admitted setup before seal, or removal attempted after seal | Module/type references remain independently owned; eligibility result | Ownership survives; prohibited later mutation invalidates |
> | A6 | C+I | Live P0/P1 with metadata anchor present | Bundle identity and owned type/module chain | Metadata remains live |
> | A7 | N+O | Unsupported unload/finalization artifact or entry request | Pre-entry profile gate | Rejection; does not simulate safe use after teardown |
> | B1 | C+I | Exact current unbound Association | Publication member-store marker; fully initialized cell | One binding published |
> | B2 | C+I | Two admitted calls serialized at native-entry barrier | First publication, second bound check | First wins; second rejects before allocation |
> | B3 | C+Q | Reentry before outer native entry | Inner publication and outer bound rejection | Same one-shot outcome; pre-E3 only |
> | B4 | C+D | Published binding in B2/B3/BF/B4 | Exact repeated-bind gate | Reject each; no retrieval/replacement |
> | B5 | C+D | Foreign Registry/work/Association/profile operands | Exact public binding-validation gate | Reject before root acquisition; interface claim |
> | B6 | C+N+I | Native allocation failure before root acquisition | Allocation-failure marker, no root/publication | Clean failure; B0 remains |
> | B7 | C+N+I | Failure after root acquisition | Root acquisition and ER0 discharge markers | Clean disposal; no source effect |
> | B8 | C+N+I | Failure after metadata binding | Candidate disposal; shared bundle unchanged | No per-cell metadata decrement |
> | B9 | C+N+I | Handle allocation failure or later prepublication failure | Root discharge, private-handle/native disposal | No partial binding |
> | B10 | C+I+O | Interruption after native publication returns | Canonical edge remains bound | Retained publication; no rollback inference |
> | B11 | C+D | Installation request before publication | Installation binding gate | Reject; zero source effect |
> | B12 | C+I | Valid publication; installation eligibility check only | Exact binding/profile/current-edge checks | Eligibility only, not installation success |
> | C1 | C+I | Live handle, B2, no P0 | Actual P0 CAS succeeds | One root-capable pin |
> | C2 | C+I | P0 already active; contender acknowledges attempt | Busy/closed result and no-use marker | Reject contender |
> | C3 | C+I | P1 acquired from live handle, then transferred to native reader | Pin acquired before worker use | Native evidence access only |
> | C4 | C+I | P1 reader overlaps B3/Closing/B4 | Barrier overlap, acquire-read committed evidence | Reader survives; no root access |
> | C5 | C+I | Last handle lease drops while P1 exists | Last-handle marker, no destruction until reader release | Destruction delayed |
> | C6 | C+I | B4, no anchor/P0, final P1 release | Last-lease CAS and free marker | Exactly one destruction |
> | C7 | C+D | Live stale handle after B4/BF | Terminal native gate; no root-use marker | P0 rejects; P1 allowed |
> | D1 | C+I | Real enclosing success prerequisites present | Resource terminal precedes Closing | Permanent new-P0 closure |
> | D2 | C+I | Closing finalizer holds sole P0 | Sole-P0/current-edge proof | Proceed to release body |
> | D3 | C+I | D2 with no observer Python ownership | Clear/decrement/released markers | Exactly one ER0 discharge |
> | D4 | C+D+I | Release-proof helper compared with safe non-authoritative wrong edge | Intended comparison reached | Reject comparison; helper-only claim |
> | D5 | C+I | B4 plus new P0 attempt | Read-closed gate | No reopening |
> | E1 | C+I+O | Actual admitted partial/uncertain failure | BF commit and root/anchor retention | No release/retry |
> | E2 | C+D | BF plus bind/finalize attempts | Bound/BF gates | Reject; evidence-only access remains |
> | E3 | C+I+O | BF over repeated observation/GC history | Fixed storage/counters; no new lifetime allocation | Finite retention; no healthy cleanup |
> | F1 | C+I | Every listed helper with valid canonical operands | Exact helper and valid-result markers | Valid canonical-read subset |
> | F2 | D+I | Wrong exact type for each helper family | Type gate before helper | Reject without dispatch; helper/interface claim |
> | F3 | N+I | Wrong schema/table/role/bounds/alignment artifact | Named pre-read gate | Reject before raw load |
> | F4 | D+I | Safe helper fixture with NULL required slot result | NULL gate, no nested dereference | Native rejection; no malformed live memory |
> | F5 | C+I | Existing error established before canonical validation | Error-entry gate; preserved indicator at exit | Reject before semantic load |
> | F6 | N+I | Controlled helper-error return in separate helper harness | Error-detection and cleanup markers | Preserve error; harness-only error-path claim |
> | F7 | C+I | Generator/type-dict owning temporary | Acquisition and ER0 release with independent owner proof | No last-reference destruction |
> | F8 | D+I | Release precondition deliberately absent in a standalone ownership helper | ER0 eligibility gate | Reject; never perform speculative decrement |
> | F9 | D+I | Wrong dict/tuple/length/string/integer operands | Specific representation gate | Reject before dependent access |
> | F10 | N+I | Instrumentation has allocation or Python-owner differences | Artifact-equivalence audit | Ineligible; no production transfer |
> | G1 | C+I | Canonical winning cut and valid graph | Current map/Association/ledger/epoch/cell acknowledgements | Assigned K observed |
> | G2 | C+I | Lawful earlier-epoch adapter dequeue, later settlement/cut | Current adapter role and dequeue evidence | Accept minimized adapter obligation |
> | G3 | C+I | Canonically released, error-free bridge | Current role/authority/fault/terminal observations | Accept assigned bridge obligation |
> | G4 | C+I | Canonically unresolved adapter/bridge/fault | Exact relevant canonical cell observed | Reject assigned Q/U |
> | G5 | C+D+I | Stale retained identity supplied only to comparison harness | Current traversal first, comparison gate second | Reject stale comparison; not end-to-end stale-state manufacture |
> | H1 | C+I+O | Python writer native precursor waits for interval marker | GIL acquisition attempt overlaps interval | No Python writer effect before exit |
> | H2 | C+I+O | Native configuration writer attempts reservation | Attempt plus occupied reservation | Zero forbidden configuration effect |
> | H3 | C+I+O | Permitted actual signal producer during validation | Generation/native recording and protected interval | Recording allowed; processing deferred |
> | I1 | C+I | All assigned checks succeed | Actual E2 release-store marker | Immutable same-attempt witness |
> | I2 | C+I | Same finalizer and continuous reservation | Actual E3 CAS after E2 | One admission |
> | I3 | C+D | Old E2/lifetime/address record presented | Certificate/handle interface gate | Reject as authority |
> | I4 | C+I | P1 reader overlaps E2 publication | Acquire observation of commit marker and payload | No partial payload treated committed |
> | J1 | N+I+O | Production/instrumented artifacts compared | Complete equivalence record | Transfer only unchanged properties |
> | J2 | D+I | Target marker missing or wrong gate reached | Oracle evaluator receives separate target/disposition fields | INCONCLUSIVE for intended target |
> | J3 | D+I | Cleanup success after recorded candidate failure | Candidate commit precedes cleanup marker | Failure remains failure |
> | K1 | C+I+O | Parent terminates at pre-E2 native boundary | Last completed native marker | SC; no E2 inferred |
> | K2 | C+I+O | Parent terminates after E2, before E3 | E2 present; E3 absent | No admission inferred |
> | K3 | C+I+O | Process exits with BF retained | BF before exit | Physical reclamation only |
> | K4 | N+O | Corrupting/raw-load-fault artifact is not safely admissible | Artifact rejection/source audit | No deliberate corruption test required |
>
> **Not yet a valid row:** “whole E1→E2 executes without allocation/error clearing.” Its exact P1-inclusive precondition and call graph are missing. It is not disguised as F1 or a quiet-run observation.
>

> ## 41. Cross-target consistency
>
> The selected mechanisms are compatible:
>
> - Anchor ownership exists before publication.
> - GC does not govern the external root owner.
> - Private construction disposal occurs outside the semantic-validation whitelist.
> - P0 closure does not tear down metadata.
> - P1 evidence readers require no Python metadata access.
> - Metadata ownership does not retain successful cells.
> - BF retains root and metadata while closing P0.
> - Raw loads require exactly P0, not a stronger hidden pin.
> - Root-release instrumentation owns no Registry reference.
> - Qualification barriers cannot change production memory ordering or GIL behavior.
>
> The unresolved P1-inclusive whitelist prevents a complete cross-target E1→E2 claim.
>
> ## 42. Frozen-input contradiction test
>
> | Input | Result |
> |---|---|
> | R0 | Compatible; callable discharge remains separate and earlier |
> | R1 | Compatible; no pending-state changes or new admission authority |
> | P1-ancestry | Exact ordering/API interface unresolved; no contradiction declared |
> | G1/T1 | Compatible; no evaluator mutation |
> | N1-PROFILER | Compatible; no new profiler |
> | Q2/QC1/C1 | Compatible; qualification separation retained |
> | S3 PASS | Preserved; future handle/slot artifacts require their own identities |
> | Integrity/minimization PASS | Preserved; no added adapter-epoch equality |
> | Frozen F2 | Preserved; no copied restoration algorithm |
> | Case F | Preserved as failed/unresolved candidate restoration |
> | Missing V4 | Preserved; not used as reconstructed authority |
>
> ## 43. Trust-boundary result
>
> The proposed taxonomy remains:
>
> | Boundary | Responsibility |
> |---|---|
> | L6/C2 | Finite signal-registration state access |
> | G1/T1 | Bounded evaluator identity observation |
> | P1-ancestry | Public live-ancestry inspection |
> | S3 | Bootstrap schema/metadata authentication |
> | CS-R1 reads | Finite current canonical-storage observation |
> | **CS-R1/LT** | Explicit anchor, handle, native leases, pins and read closure |
> | Canonical binding writer | One authenticated future Association binding |
> | POSIX/macOS | Source configuration operations |
>
> CS-R1/LT names the formerly incomplete native lifetime machinery; it does not add arbitrary interpreter access or semantic ownership. The binding setter is confined to canonical construction, not included in the read-only E1→E2 boundary.
>
> No new semantic governance was shown necessary.
>

>

> ## 48. Independent re-review readiness
>
> The B2.1/B2.2 candidate can be independently challenged as written, but the integrated four-target package is **not ready** to claim complete correction.
>
> The smallest remaining question is:
>
> > What is the exact frozen P1 public-frame call/release sequence relative to E1, including whether frame materialization can occur after E1 acquisition?
>
> **Next work: read-only analysis.**
>
> Minimum evidence:
>
> - The preserved P1 contract/report with exact E1 ordering.
> - Its complete public-frame API and IR0 release inventory.

> ## 3. Exact P1 ordering
>
> The recovered contract establishes this ordering:

> 4. Public current-thread ancestry inspection and canonical-code comparisons.

> Linearization points:
>

> | E1 reservation | Successful owner/attempt-bound native CAS |
> | Ancestry completion | Complete traversal with clean terminal NULL and no prohibited match |
> | E2 | Validation publication after ancestry, semantic validation and inspection-reference discharge |

> The original transition description says:

> ## 4. Interval definitions
>
> | Interval | Meaning |
> |---|---|
> | I0 | Before provisional reservation |
> | I1 | E1 held; ancestry validation incomplete |
> | I2 | Ancestry complete; canonical semantic validation incomplete |
> | I3 | Semantic validation complete through E2 publication |
> | I4 | E2 published through E3 |

> E1-A would correspond to ancestry inspection **plus its completed cleanup**. That cleanup-inclusive endpoint is the unresolved historical detail.
