> E1-B would begin only after that endpoint. Neither name creates another reservation or admission state.
>
> ## 5. Public frame API audit
>
> All findings below concern pinned **CPython v3.14.5**, GIL-enabled execution, valid live inputs and the admitted runtime-hook profile.
>
> | Operation | Declaration / classification | Ownership and behavior |
> |---|---|---|
> | `PyThreadState_Get()` | Public C API | Borrowed attached thread state; invalid attachment is not a recoverable scanner input |
> | `PyThreadState_GetInterpreter(tstate)` | Public C API | Borrowed identity; no allocation or Python dispatch |
> | `PyThread_get_thread_ident()` | Public C API | Native scalar identity; runtime/thread subsystem already initialized |
> | `PyThreadState_GetFrame(tstate)` | `Include/pystate.h`; Stable ABI since 3.10 | New frame reference; may materialize; initial NULL always rejects |
> | `PyFrame_GetBack(frame)` | `Include/cpython/pyframe.h`; public full API, not Limited/Stable | New reference or NULL; may materialize an ancestor |
> | `PyFrame_GetCode(frame)` | `Include/pyframe.h`; Stable ABI since 3.10 | New code reference; no materialization or allocating lookup on valid input |
> | `Py_TYPE` and pointer comparisons | Public C operations | No owning result, coercion, descriptor or rich comparison |
> | `Py_DECREF` | Public reference operation | Safe here only through the specific IR0 non-last proof |
> | `PyErr_Occurred()` | Public C API | Borrowed error observation |
> | `PyErr_GetRaisedException()` | Public; Stable ABI since 3.12 | Transfers error-indicator ownership to the caller |
> | `PyErr_SetRaisedException(exc)` | Public; Stable ABI since 3.12 | Transfers ownership back; safe here only with an empty destination indicator |
>
> The public frame getters do not themselves release the GIL or process signals/pending calls on these admitted paths. `PyFrame_GetCode`’s critical-section macros do not release the GIL in this build configuration. [Pinned declarations](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Include/pyframe.h), [frame implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/frameobject.c), [Stable ABI inventory](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Misc/stable_abi.toml)
>
> Internal implementations were inspected as evidence; no internal frame accessor is proposed for candidate use.
>
> ## 6. Materialization behavior
>
> Initial and ancestor frame acquisition can reach `_PyFrame_MakeAndSetFrameObject` and `_PyFrame_New_NoTrack`. Code retrieval from an existing valid frame does not materialize another frame.
>
> The allocation path:
>
> - Can allocate frame storage.
> - Does not execute Python or run GC under the admitted allocator/runtime profile.
> - Does not unwind frames, release the GIL or process pending calls.
> - Can produce `MemoryError`.
> - Can **schedule later GC** by setting the GC scheduling bit; scheduling is distinct from running collection.
>
> Thus “no GC” must mean no collection execution during the protected interval, not an unchanged GC allocation counter or scheduling bit. [Frame materialization](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/frame.c), [GC allocation and scheduling](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/gc.c)
>
> This is not valid for arbitrary allocator or reference-tracer hooks. Object allocation dispatches through configured allocator machinery, and new-reference processing includes the reference-tracer path. Those dependencies must remain within the previously required authenticated hook profile. [Allocator dispatch](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/obmalloc.c), [reference initialization](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/object.c)
>
> ## 7. Initial-frame failure
>
> `PyThreadState_GetFrame` clears the error when frame materialization returns NULL. A NULL result can therefore lack an error indicator.
>
> The recovered P1 rule is correct:
>
> - Reject initial NULL.
> - Record **initial ancestry unavailable**.
> - Do not infer empty ancestry.
> - Do not fabricate an OOM diagnosis.
> - Do not enter canonical validation or publish E2/E3.
> - Release any acquired reservation before returning.
>
> A clear error indicator before inspection is essential: otherwise materialization could dispose of a previously saved exception on failure. [Pinned thread-state getter](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/pystate.c)
>
> The internal clearing of its newly obtained built-in `MemoryError` is a separately bounded cleanup path, not a general permission to clear arbitrary exceptions.
>
> ## 8. Back-frame failure
>
> After a valid initial frame:
>
> - `PyFrame_GetBack` returning NULL with a clear error indicator means clean ancestry exhaustion.
> - NULL with an error means failed traversal; reject without accepting the scanned prefix.
>
> The pinned implementation can propagate ancestor-materialization failure, despite the documentation’s broader “raises no exceptions” wording. The implementation therefore controls this exact-build failure analysis. [Implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/frameobject.c), [pinned documentation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Doc/c-api/frame.rst)
>
> On failure, P1 transfers the error out, releases IR0 references and E1, then restores the same error without formatting.
>
> ## 9. IR0 ownership/release
>
> | Reference | Independent owner | Established release rule |
> |---|---|---|
> | Initial frame | Its live interpreter frame owns the materialized frame object | After acquiring the next frame, or during rejection cleanup |
> | Each live ancestor | Its own live interpreter/generator frame | Same traversal discipline |
> | Returned code | Live frame’s executable ownership | Immediately after identity comparison |
> | Inspection exception | Ownership transferred from/to error indicator | No destructive scanner release |
> | Thread/interpreter identities | Runtime-owned, borrowed | No DECREF |
>
> Under retained GIL, no Python execution and no teardown, these live frames cannot unwind during scanning. Their scanner references therefore cannot be last references. Completed frames and suspended generators supplied independently are not valid substitutes for current ancestry.
>
> The unresolved point is narrower than this ownership proof: **when the final current-frame reference is released after clean ancestry exhaustion, relative to semantic validation**.
>
> The source proves that an earlier non-last release can be safe. It cannot prove that earlier release was the historically selected P1 sequence.
>
> ## 10. Error-state interaction
>
> | Case | Preserved treatment |
> |---|---|
> | Error already set | Reject before E1 and inspection; preserve unchanged |
> | Initial materialization fails and clears error | Fixed native rejection; no invented exception |
> | Ancestor materialization fails | Transfer error out, IR0 cleanup, E1 release, transfer back |
> | Valid code getter unexpectedly fails | Reject; never treat failure as completed inspection |
> | Canonical match without error | Native rejection reason; no formatting inside reservation |
> | Cleanup with an inspection error | Transfer ownership rather than destructively clear it |
>
> `PyErr_SetRaisedException` decrements an existing destination exception. Its P1 use therefore requires the destination to remain empty. [Error ownership implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/errors.c)
>
> The initial failure’s built-in `MemoryError` comes from the cleared freelist or retained last-resort object. Its disposal is bounded by that exact construction, not merely by its exception class name. [MemoryError implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/exceptions.c)
>

> This remains conditional on the authenticated GIL-enabled build and permitted allocator/runtime hooks. It is not runtime qualification and does not establish the disputed cleanup ordering.

> ## 13. Reservation-purpose analysis
>
> E1 protects the native configuration-writer slot and orders competing finalization attempts. It is already held during P1 scanning.
>
> It does not independently:
>
> - Keep native-cell storage alive—that belongs to live-handle/pin ownership.
> - Prevent Python execution—that requires retained GIL and callback exclusion.
> - Stop runtime signal recording.
>
> Moving the scan before E1 would alter the selected native-writer ordering and would not be a naming correction.
>
> ## 14. GIL-purpose analysis
>
> Retaining the GIL, while executing no Python, prevents another Python thread from beginning canonical mutation and prevents the current Python ancestry from advancing or unwinding.
>
> It supports both live-frame ownership and later canonical-container stability.
>
> It does not exclude unrelated native configuration writers or runtime signal producers. E1 excludes the former; the latter remain allowed to record pending state.
>

>
> | Property | I0 | I1: ancestry | I2: canonical | I3: E2 | I4: E3 |
> |---|---|---|---|---|---|
> | No Python callback/bytecode | Before-entry rules | Required | Required | Required | Required |
> | No GC/weakref/finalizer execution | Before-entry rules | Required | Required | Required | Required |
> | GIL continuously held | Established before inspection | Required | Required | Required | Required |
> | No signal checking/pending-call processing | Before-entry rules | Required | Required | Required | Required |
> | No canonical Python writer | Not inferred from E1 | GIL/no-callback | GIL/no-callback | Same | Same |
> | No native configuration writer | Reservation not yet held | E1 | Same E1 | Same E1 | Same E1 |

> | No callback-capable last release | Required for protected cleanup | IR0/bounded internal error path | ER0 | Same | Same |
>
> P1 needs stable live ancestry. CS-R1 needs stable canonical owning edges. E2/E3 need continuous exclusion and same-attempt publication. Qualification must observe these separately; allocation-freedom cannot substitute for them.

> ## 3. T1 analysis
>
> T1 is viable.
>
> After complete, clean ancestry exhaustion, the scanner no longer needs its final frame. Releasing its owning reference then:
>
> - Preserves the IR0 independent-owner proof.
> - Leaves ancestry evidence valid under continuous exclusion.
> - Removes scanner ownership from every later semantic path.
> - Gives MX an explicit cleanup-complete boundary.
> - Keeps P1-specific operations outside the canonical H1 interval.
>
> No semantic-validation input requires the released scanner reference.
>
> ## 4. T2 analysis
>
> T2 is also technically viable under the historical constraints.
>
> The final live frame remains independently anchored throughout callback-free, GIL-held semantic validation. Its later decrement can remain IR0 and preserve the error indicator.
>
> However, T2 requires:
>
> - Adding the retained frame to the canonical interval’s ownership inventory.
> - Releasing it on semantic success, rejection and unexpected helper-error paths.
> - Distinguishing semantic completion from subsequent scanner cleanup.
> - Preventing E2 until that additional cleanup finishes.
>
> T2 is not rejected as unsafe. It offers no necessary semantic benefit and creates a larger cleanup obligation.
>

> ## 6. Minimum-lifetime principle
>
> Adopt prospectively, specifically for P1 inspection temporaries:
>
> > Release an inspection-owned reference when inspection no longer needs it, while its established IR0 independent-owner proof remains valid.
>
> This completes reference-lifetime architecture. It does not change canonical state, ancestry detection, reservation semantics or semantic authority.
>
> It does not become a general rule permitting early release of other ownership classes. No governance change is required. The completed candidate still requires independent HIGH-risk review.
>
> ## 7. Selected T1/T2 decision
>
> **Select OPTION T1 — RELEASE AT SCANNER COMPLETION.**
>
> Freeze this successful sequence:
>
> 1. Inspect and compare the current frame’s code.
> 2. Release the returned code reference immediately after comparison.
> 3. Acquire the back-frame result while retaining the current frame.
> 4. For a valid successor, release the predecessor reference and continue.
> 5. For terminal NULL, establish that the error indicator is clear.
> 6. Release the final current-frame reference exactly once under IR0.
> 7. Establish that all scanner-owned frame/code slots are empty and no inspection error is owned.
> 8. Complete E1-A.
> 9. Enter canonical E1-B under the same E1/P0/GIL.
> 10. Complete canonical validation and its required temporary cleanup.
> 11. Publish E2.
> 12. Permit E3 only through the separately constrained same-attempt admission transition.
>
> No scanner-owned reference survives step 7.
>
> ## 8. Selected-option safety proof
>
> At the terminal decrement:
>
> - Clean ancestry exhaustion has already been established.
> - The final frame belongs to current live ancestry.
> - Its executing interpreter/generator frame still independently owns the materialized frame object.
> - No Python execution, GIL release or teardown has occurred.
> - The scanner decrement therefore cannot be last.
> - No frame destruction, locals destruction, weakref/finalizer execution or collection follows from that decrement.
> - A non-last decrement does not change the Python error indicator.
> - E1 and P0 remain held.
>
> The pinned ownership transfer and clearing paths support that independent ownership; the public getters supply separate owning inspection references. [Frame ownership](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/frame.c), [public getter implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Objects/frameobject.c)
>
> Ancestry does not become stale when the scanner reference is released: continuous GIL/no-callback execution prevents Python ancestry changes, while E1 continues excluding native configuration writers.
>
> No separate persistent native state bit is required.
>
> ## 9. Error-state consequence
>
> | Case | T1 | T2 |
> |---|---|---|
> | P1 success, clear error | Final IR0 released before semantic entry | Final frame retained |
> | Semantic success | No scanner cleanup remains | Release final IR0 before E2 |
> | Semantic rejection without error | Canonical cleanup only | Canonical cleanup plus retained frame |
> | Unexpected semantic helper error | Preserve error; canonical cleanup only | Preserve error while also releasing frame |
> | Crash/SC | No inferred cleanup beyond observed evidence | Same limitation |
>
> For selected T1, E1-B requires a clear error indicator at entry. An unexpected helper error terminates validation; it does not become an accepted error-mutating path.
>
> P1’s existing rejection-side error transfer remains unchanged. `PyErr_SetRaisedException` is safe for that transfer only with an empty destination indicator, because replacing an existing exception can decrement it. [Pinned error implementation](https://github.com/python/cpython/blob/v3.14.5/Python/errors.c)
>
> ## 10. Failure-path consequence
>
> | Boundary | Frozen treatment |
> |---|---|
> | Active canonical frame | Stop inspection; release code/frame IR0; fixed rejection evidence; release E1 before Python resumes |
> | Initial-frame NULL | Reject as ancestry unavailable; no semantic entry; no fabricated OOM diagnosis |
> | Ancestor failure | Preserve the inspection error through the existing transfer discipline; IR0 cleanup; synchronous reservation release |
> | Ancestry success, semantic rejection | IR0 is already complete; release canonical ER0 temporaries, record rejection and release reservation |
> | Ancestry success, helper error | Same, preserving the error; no protected formatting, normalization or replacement |
> | Failure before E2 publication | No E2/E3; intact-runtime rejection cleanup applies |
> | Uncertainty about whether E2 published | No inferred E3; classify uncertainty/SC according to the existing publication contract |
> | Crash after ancestry success, before semantic entry | Only independently recorded evidence survives; no guaranteed cleanup |
> | Crash during semantic validation | No inferred E2, E3, restoration or reservation release |
>
> An ordinary intact-runtime publication path uses preallocated native storage. Corruption or uncertain execution must not be disguised as a clean publication failure.
>
> No precommit rejection performs source finalization.
>

>
> Adopt these prospective names:
>
> **E1-A:** reserved ancestry validation, including complete IR0 cleanup.
> **E1-B:** canonical CS-R1 validation under the allocation-free, error-preserving H1 whitelist.

>
> - Complete successful ancestry traversal.
> - No prohibited canonical match.

> ## 12. H1 final scope
>
> Adopt:
>
> > The protected admission operation contains two consecutive audited callback-free regions under one continuous E1 reservation, P0 pin and GIL. P1 ancestry validation may perform its exact-build bounded frame materialization, which may allocate but does not execute Python or run GC in the admitted profile. P1 completes by discharging all IR0 inspection references. Canonical CS-R1 validation then executes its exact allocation-free, error-preserving H1 whitelist. Neither region permits Python execution, Python callback/finalizer/weakref execution, GC collection, GIL release, signal/pending processing or admission of an excluded writer.

> - Runtime signal recording remains allowed.

> - P1 retains its distinct failure/error-transfer contract.
> - “Error-preserving” describes the admitted canonical path. Unexpected helper errors cause rejection and preservation, not continued validation.
> - The exact allocator/runtime-hook restrictions remain required.
>
> The canonical whitelist remains the preceding candidate: exact identity checks; audited dict/tuple/Unicode/integer operations; selected type/function/generator getters; `PyErr_Occurred`; specified ER0 decrements; authenticated fixed-size slot loading; checked native arithmetic, evidence stores and atomics.
>
> P1 frame getters and error-transfer operations are **not** added to E1-B.

> ## 14. Qualification delta
>
> These are prospective rows only. All use the inherited exact-artifact, isolated-process, **12-second deadline**, evidence-before-cleanup and PASS/FAIL/INCONCLUSIVE rules.
>
> | Row | Construction and synchronization | Positive oracle |
> |---|---|---|
> | A. Exhaustion → final release | Canonical no-match ancestry; native markers around terminal NULL/error check and final decrement | Clean exhaustion precedes exactly one final IR0 discharge |
> | B. Release → canonical entry | Same invocation; ordered scalar markers after decrement and at E1-B entry | E1-B follows zero outstanding IR0 ownership; same attempt/E1/P0 |
> | C. Semantic rejection | Canonical history reaches an existing false semantic predicate; no direct fixture mutation | E1-A passes, E1-B rejects, no E2/E3; no scanner cleanup remains |
> | D. Semantic helper error | Separately authenticated negative artifact supplies a fixed pre-owned exception at one designated helper-failure boundary | Intended failure branch reached; error preserved; canonical cleanup and rejection; no scanner reference |
> | E. Crash between regions | Parent waits for completed-IR0 marker; separately qualified native barrier precedes E1-B; parent terminates candidate | Containment plus observed markers only; no inferred E1-B/E2/E3 |
> | F. Continuous ownership | Instrument actual E1/P0 acquisition/release sites and region boundaries; source/artifact audit covers GIL-retention path | One uninterrupted E1/P0 ownership interval; no detach/reacquisition |
> | G. Writer across transition | Native writer signals readiness, waits for transition window, performs actual reservation attempt and publishes its result | Attempt witnessed inside held E1; zero configuration effect |
> | H. Actual signal across transition | Parent/native producer is released by transition-window marker; actual signal recording acknowledged natively | Recording occurred; no Python delivery or pending-state processing in the transition |
> | I. No callback | Native entry observation plus audited call graph; no Python trace hook in success path | Transition reached; any forbidden callback entry is FAIL |
> | J. No GC/finalizer | Native observation of forbidden execution paths plus source proof | Collection/finalizer execution is FAIL; deferred GC scheduling is separately classified |
> | K. E2 ordering | Ordered evidence for A-complete, B-entry, B-result, canonical cleanup and E2 commit | E2 follows both successful regions and cleanup; rejection histories contain no E2 |
> | L. Observer does not retain frame | Audit instrumentation reference operations and compare production/instrumented ownership paths | No observer INCREF, Python frame capture, GC edge or retained frame object |
>
> Additional limits:
>
> - Row D tests the caller’s error branch; it does not prove the production getter naturally fails under valid preconditions or transfer the negative helper into the production whitelist.
> - Native barriers must not release the GIL or alter real reservation/pin operations.
> - Python-writer contention must be witnessed as a GIL-acquisition attempt, not claimed as concurrent Python execution.
> - Ownership discharge markers follow the actual decrement operation; merely clearing a local pointer is insufficient evidence.
> - Callback/GC absence combines source proof with observed histories, not experimental proof of a universal negative.
> - Instrumentation differences remain fixed scalar evidence and native synchronization only. They may not extend frame lifetime.
> - Cleanup never upgrades a missing or failed target.
>
> These close the **transition-specific architecture oracle**, without executing qualification.
